Prompt · The question’s wording
-------------------------------

- 1 Define the term in the question: "the 2024 Legacy Rule amendments that EPA identifies in these notices as applying to all CCR units (both the interpretive amendments EPA approves as State versions and the more prescriptive amendments the States did not adopt)". Why: The phrase "all-unit amendments" is doing hidden work: each notice splits the 2024 Legacy Rule changes into (a) amendments EPA says "apply to all CCR units" whose State versions EPA is approving, and (b) a second all-unit set of "more prescriptive" amendments the States did not adopt. Solvers must count across BOTH lists. Low model answers of 0 and 3 show it read only one list (e.g., #4 counted only the three definitional amendments Alabama alone lacks). From a run (Low model · attempt 4): Counted only the three definitions Alabama uniquely lacks ("Contains both CCR and liquids," "Infiltration," "Liquids"), ignoring Wisconsin's 11-item approved list, which omits nine further all-unit amendments Virginia and Louisiana have. How answers are graded

- Each attempt is compared with your answer and marked right or wrong. The grade is on the whole attempt; the final answer shown is picked out automatically as a guide.

- For an ordered list, a title, honorific or article added to an entry is accepted if the order is right either with it or without it.
- Only the value the attempt finally commits to counts. Values reached while working, or considered and set aside, do not. An attempt that offers several values without choosing one is wrong, even if one of them is right.

Answer: 0

Reasoning

The five proposed approvals are for Wyoming, Virginia, Louisiana, Alabama, and Wisconsin (Docs 2‑6). In each proposal, EPA separates the 2024 Legacy Rule’s amendments that apply to all CCR units into two groups:

1. Provisions the State has adopted/interpreted (e.g., applicability updates in §257.50(c), definition changes for “CCR unit,” “infiltration,” “liquids,” “state director,” etc., and updates to §§257.61(a), 257.80(a), 257.90(a), 257.100(a)(1), 257.104(a)). These are present in all five proposed programs except for a few (such as the “contains both CCR and liquids,” “infiltration,” and “liquids” definitions) that are missing only in Alabama.
2. Provisions the State did not adopt—the more prescriptive 2024 amendments:

- “Operator” and “Owner” definitions (§257.53)
- §257.80(b)(6)
- §257.102(c)(2), (d)(2), (f)(2)(ii)(C) & (D)
- §257.104(a)(2), (c)(1) & (3), (g)
- §257.105 updates
- §257.107 updates

These are explicitly listed as not adopted / not approved in Virginia, Louisiana, Alabama, and Wisconsin—four States—while Wyoming’s program is treated as meeting the standard for them (Wyoming’s proposal lists only four other exclusions).

Other all‑unit provisions that are excluded (e.g., vegetative‑cover requirements in §§257.73/257.74, groundwater‑monitoring suspension in §257.90(g), and groundwater‑protection standards in §257.95(h)(2)) are absent in four or all five States.

Because every 2024 all‑unit amendment is either:

- present in all (or all but one) State, or
- absent in four States (Virginia, Louisiana, Alabama, Wisconsin),

no 2024 Legacy Rule all‑unit amendment lacks an approved State version in exactly two States.

Full answer
-----------

The 2024 Legacy Rule made two kinds of changes: (1) provisions that apply to all CCR units, and (2) provisions that apply only to legacy surface impoundments or CCR management units (CCRMUs).

Across the five proposed approvals (Wyoming, Virginia, Louisiana, Alabama, and Wisconsin), the states explicitly list the same core set of “all‑unit” 2024 amendments that they have not adopted or are not seeking approval for:

- 40 CFR 257.90(g) (suspension of groundwater monitoring)
- 40 CFR 257.95(h)(2) (groundwater standards for constituents with no MCL)
- The Group B “all‑unit” revisions (the “Operator”/“Owner” definitions in § 257.53; § 257.80(b)(6); § 257.102(c)(2), (d)(2), and (f)(2)(ii)(C)–(D); § 257.104(a)(2), (c)(1) and (3), and (g); § 257.105(a) and its retention provisions; and § 257.107(b) and its retention provisions).

Checking each state’s “not included” lists:

- Wyoming lists 257.90(g) and 257.95(h)(2) (and the legacy/impoundment‑specific items).
- Virginia, Louisiana, Alabama, and Wisconsin each list 257.90(g), 257.95(h)(2), and the full Group B set.

So every one of those all‑unit amendments is missing in four or five of the proposed programs (e.g., the Group B items are absent in Virginia, Louisiana, Alabama, and Wisconsin; 257.90(g) and 257.95(h)(2) are absent in all five).

The other 2024 “all‑unit” amendments (e.g., § 257.50(c), § 257.52, the updated definitions for “CCR unit,” “inactive CCR surface impoundment,” “infiltration,” “liquids,” etc., § 257.61(a), § 257.80(a), § 257.90(a), § 257.100(a)(1), and § 257.104(a)) are either incorporated by reference or interpreted by the States as part of their approved programs and therefore are covered.

Because every 2024 all‑unit amendment either:

- is covered in all (or nearly all) five programs, or
- is missing in four or five of them,

there is no 2024 Legacy Rule all‑unit amendment that lacks an approved State version in exactly two States.

Full answer
-----------

Answer: 0

Reasoning

The five proposed approvals cover Wyoming, Virginia, Louisiana, Alabama, and Wisconsin. In each proposal EPA lists the 2024 Legacy Rule amendments that apply to all CCR units and identifies which ones the State has (or has not) incorporated:

- All five States adopted the same core set of 20 all-unit provisions (e.g., the revised applicability in §257.50(c), the reserved §257.51, the updated definitions in §257.53, the fugitive-dust change in §257.80(a), the groundwater-monitoring applicability in §257.90(a), the closure/post-closure updates in §§257.100(a)(1) and 257.104(a), etc.).
- All five States also failed to adopt/incorporate the same remaining all-unit amendments—specifically the 12 provisions in the “did not adopt these all-unit amendments” lists (the updated “Operator”/“Owner” definitions, §257.80(b)(6), the closure/removal criteria in §§257.102(c)(2), (d)(2), (f)(2)(ii)(C)-(D), the post-closure care updates in §§257.104(a)(2), (c)(1), (c)(3), (g), and the recordkeeping/retention-time updates in §§257.105 and 257.107) plus §§257.90(g) and 257.95(h)(2). The vegetative-cover provisions (§§257.73/257.74) are also absent from all five approved programs.

The only variations among the five are:

- Louisiana explicitly excludes §257.51 (missing in 1 State).
- Alabama does not seek approval for its analog to §257.103(f) (missing in 1 State).
- Wisconsin seeks approval only for CCR landfills, so all CCR-surface-impoundment provisions remain federally enforceable, but those are not “all-unit” amendments.

Because the same 12+ all-unit amendments are missing in all five States, and the remaining all-unit amendments are present in four or five States, no 2024 Legacy Rule all-unit amendment is missing in exactly two States.

Hence the number is 0 (none).

Full answer

Looking at the five proposed approvals—Wyoming (Doc 2), Virginia (Doc 3), Louisiana (Doc 4), Alabama (Doc 5), and Wisconsin (Doc 6)—EPA lists the 2024 Legacy Rule “all‑unit” amendments that each State has not adopted/seeks approval for.

The amendments that apply to all CCR units fall into two groups in every proposal:

- The 20 “baseline” amendments (e.g., updated definitions, applicability, fugitive‑dust, groundwater‑monitoring applicability, closure/post‑closure language, recordkeeping/website updates).
- The additional “more prescriptive” 2024 amendments (e.g., updated “Operator”/“Owner” definitions, 40 CFR 257.80(b)(6), 257.102(c)(2) and (d)(2), 257.104(g), 257.105/107 retention rules).

Across the five States:

- 90(g) and 95(h)(2) are missing in all five.
- The vegetative‑cover provisions (257.73/74) and the “more prescriptive” group are missing in four States (Virginia, Louisiana, Alabama, Wisconsin) but present in Wyoming.
- By contrast, the three new 2024 definitions—“Contains both CCR and liquids,” “Infiltration,” and “Liquids”—are explicitly listed in the State‑approved sets for Wyoming, Virginia, and Louisiana, but are not included in Alabama’s application (Doc 5, “Second” list) and are absent from Wisconsin’s pre‑2024 incorporated rules (Doc 6, incorporated only through Aug 2020). No other State lists them as missing.

So those three definition amendments lack an approved State version in exactly two States (Alabama and Wisconsin).

Answer: 3 (the three 2024 Legacy Rule definitional amendments: “Contains both CCR and liquids,” “Infiltration,” and “Liquids”).

Reasoning

The 2024 Legacy Rule made two kinds of changes that apply to all CCR units (not just legacy impoundments or CCR management units):

1. The “core” 20 updates (e.g., revised definitions, scope clarifications, updates to §§ 257.50(c), 257.52, 257.61(a), 257.80(a), 257.90(a), 257.100(a)(1), 257.104(a), etc.).

- Wyoming, Virginia, Louisiana, and Alabama each list these 20 provisions as being covered by their State programs.
- Wisconsin incorporated the pre‑2020 federal landfill rules and then updated its own code for CCR landfills; its application does not exclude these 20 provisions, so they remain in Wisconsin’s approved analogs.

1. The remaining all‑unit amendments added in 2024 (e.g., §§ 257.73/74 vegetative‑cover provisions, 257.90(g) suspension of monitoring, 257.95(h)(2) groundwater standards for cobalt/lead/lithium/molybdenum, plus the Wisconsin‑specific omissions such as the “Operator”/“Owner” definitions, §§ 257.80(b)(6), 257.102(c)(2), 257.102(d)(2), 257.104(g), 257.105(a), etc.).

Looking at what each State explicitly excludes from its approved program:

| State | Explicitly excluded all‑unit 2024 amendments | |-------|----------------------------------------------| | Wyoming | 257.90(g), 257.95(h)(2) | | Virginia | 257.90(g), 257.95(h)(2) | | Louisiana | 257.90(g), 257.95(h)(2) | | Alabama | 257.90(g), 257.95(h)(2) | | Wisconsin | 257.73/74, 257.90(g), 257.95(h)(2), plus Operator/Owner, 257.80(b)(6), 257.102(c)(2), 257.102(d)(2), 257.102(f)(2)(ii)(C,D), 257.104(a)(2)/(c)(1)/(3), 257.104(g), 257.105(a) and retention extensions, 257.107(b) and retention extensions |

So:

- 257.90(g) and 257.95(h)(2) are missing in all five States.
- 257.73/74 (vegetative cover) is missing in all five States (though it is technically impoundment‑focused, it is listed in every State’s exclusions).
- Every other 2024 all‑unit amendment (the “Wisconsin‑only” group) is missing in only Wisconsin (1 State).

Because no 2024 all‑unit amendment is absent in exactly two States—each is either absent in all five or absent only in Wisconsin—the number that lack an approved State version in exactly two States is zero.

In Document 3 (Virginia), under section "3. Virginia Partial Program", there's a list of amendments from the 2024 Legacy Rule that EPA is approving for Virginia:

"1. Throughout 40 CFR part 257, subpart D, the regulations were amended by removing the phrase ``website'' and adding in its place the word ``website'' wherever it appears.
2. 40 CFR 257.50(c); this amendment revises the scope of applicability to specify that it includes inactive CCR surface impoundments at utilities or power producers regardless of how electricity is currently being produced at the facility.
3. 40 CFR 257.51; this section was reserved, as the effective date of 40 CFR part 257, subpart D, October 19, 2015, has passed.
4. 40 CFR 257.52; this amendment clarifies that all CCR units are subject to the requirement to comply with all other Federal, State, Tribal, or local laws or other requirements. In addition, all CCR units continue to be subject to 40 CFR 257.3-1, 257.3-2, and 257.3-3.
5. ``Active facility or active electric utilities or independent power producers''; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that the relevant operational date for any active facility or active electric utilities or independent power producers is on or after October 19, 2015.
6. ``CCR landfill or landfill''; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that a CCR landfill means an area of land or an excavation that ``contains'', rather than ``receives'', CCR, and meets the other criteria of the definition.
7. ``CCR surface impoundment or impoundment''; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 deleted the words ``which is''.
8. ``CCR unit''; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that this term includes legacy CCR surface impoundments and CCRMU.
9. ``Contains both CCR and liquids''; this additional definition in 40 CFR 257.53 is consistent with the term's plain meaning and dictionary definitions as this term used in the closure performance standard in 40 CFR 257.102(d)(2)(i) for CCR surface impoundments.
10. ``Inactive CCR surface impoundment'', this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that this term is applicable to such CCR surface impoundments ``located at an active facility.''
11. ``Infiltration''; this additional definition in 40 CFR 257.53 is consistent with the term's plain meaning and dictionary definitions to assist in the application of closure performance standards for CCR units.
12. ``Liquids''; this additional definition in 40 CFR 257.53 is consistent with the term's plain meaning and dictionary definitions to assist in the applicability for CCR surface impoundments and the application of closure performance standards for CCR units.
13. ``State director''; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that the State director is the chief administrative officer of the lead State agency responsible for implementing the State program regulating disposal in all CCR units.
14. ``Technically feasible or feasible''; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that certain requirements of 40 CFR part 257, subpart D refer only to feasible rather than technically feasible. The amendment ensures that these terms are interpreted in the same way.
15. ``Technically infeasible or infeasible''; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that certain requirements of 40 CFR part 257, subpart D refer only to infeasible rather than technically infeasible. The amendment ensures that these terms are interpreted in the same way.
16. 40 CFR 257.61(a); this amendment updates a reference to 40 CFR 230.41(a), as the previously referenced provision has since been amended.
17. 40 CFR 257.80(a); this amendment clarifies that all CCR units are subject to the fugitive dust requirements.
18. 40 CFR 257.90(a); this amendment clarifies that all CCR units are subject to the groundwater monitoring and corrective action requirements. In
addition, it corrects a typographical error.
19. 40 CFR 257.100(a)(1); this amendment clarifies that inactive CCR surface impoundments, regardless of how the facility produces electricity through non-fuels, are subject to the same compliance deadlines applicable to existing CCR surface impoundments, subject to certain requirements.
20. 40 CFR 257.104(a); this amendment clarifies that all owners or operators of CCR units that are subject to 40 CFR 257.102 are subject to the post-closure care requirements, except for those owners and operators of a CCR unit that elect to close the CCR unit by removing CCR."

This appears to be a list of 20 amendments from the 2024 Legacy Rule that apply to all CCR units.

Looking at this question, I need to identify the 2024 Legacy Rule's "all-unit amendments" and determine which ones lack an approved State version in exactly 2 of the 5 proposed State program approvals (Wyoming, Virginia, Louisiana, Alabama, Wisconsin).

Identifying the All-Unit Amendments

Based on the EPA analyses in the State approval documents, the 2024 Legacy Rule's all-unit amendments fall into two groups:

Group A (20 items): Amendments EPA is "approving the State's version of" because the State interprets its provisions consistent with EPA's interpretation.

Group B (12 items): "More prescriptive" amendments that States did not adopt.

Analyzing Approval Status Across 5 States

Wyoming: Approved State version is the Federal CCR regulations through December 14, 2020. None of the 32 Legacy Rule all-unit amendments are approved.

Virginia & Louisiana: Both approve all 20 Group A items.

Alabama: Approves 17 Group A items (does NOT approve "Contains both CCR and liquids," "Infiltration," and "Liquids").

Wisconsin: Approves 11 Group A items (only regulates landfills, so many surface-impoundment-related items are not approved).

Let me list all 20 amendments from Virginia/Louisiana:

1. Throughout 40 CFR part 257, subpart D, the regulations were amended by removing the phrase "website" and adding in its place the word "website" wherever it appears.
2. 40 CFR 257.50(c); this amendment revises the scope of applicability to specify that it includes inactive CCR surface impoundments at utilities or power producers regardless of how electricity is currently being produced at the facility.
3. 40 CFR 257.51; this section was reserved, as the effective date of 40 CFR part 257, subpart D, October 19, 2015, has passed.
4. 40 CFR 257.52; this amendment clarifies that all CCR units are subject to the requirement to comply with all other Federal, State, Tribal, or local laws or other requirements. In addition, all CCR units continue to be subject to 40 CFR 257.3-1, 257.3-2, and 257.3-3.
5. "Active facility or active electric utilities or independent power producers"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that the relevant operational date for any active facility or active electric utilities or independent power producers is on or after October 19, 2015.
6. "CCR landfill or landfill"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that a CCR landfill means an area of land or an excavation that "contains", rather than "receives", CCR, and meets the other criteria of the definition.
7. "CCR surface impoundment or impoundment"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 deleted the words "which is".
8. "CCR unit"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that this term includes legacy CCR surface impoundments and CCRMU.
9. "Contains both CCR and liquids"; this additional definition in 40 CFR 257.53 is consistent with the term's plain meaning and dictionary definitions as this term used in the closure performance standard in 40 CFR 257.102(d)(2)(i) for CCR surface impoundments.
10. "Inactive CCR surface impoundment", this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that this term is applicable to such CCR surface impoundments "located at an active facility."
11. "Infiltration"; this additional definition in 40 CFR 257.53 is consistent with the term's plain meaning and dictionary definitions to assist in the application of closure performance standards for CCR units.
12. "Liquids"; this additional definition in 40 CFR 257.53 is consistent with the term's plain meaning and dictionary definitions to assist in the applicability for CCR surface impoundments and the application of closure performance standards for CCR units.
13. "State director"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that the State director is the chief administrative officer of the lead State agency responsible for implementing the State program regulating disposal in all CCR units.
14. "Technically feasible or feasible"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that certain requirements of 40 CFR part 257, subpart D refer only to feasible rather than technically feasible. The amendment ensures that these terms are interpreted in the same way.
15. "Technically infeasible or infeasible"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that certain requirements of 40 CFR part 257, subpart D refer only to infeasible rather than technically infeasible. The amendment ensures that these terms are interpreted in the same way.
16. 40 CFR 257.61(a); this amendment updates a reference to 40 CFR 230.41(a), as the previously referenced provision has since been amended.
17. 40 CFR 257.80(a); this amendment clarifies that all CCR units are subject to the fugitive dust requirements.
18. 40 CFR 257.90(a); this amendment clarifies that all CCR units are subject to the groundwater monitoring and corrective action requirements. In addition, it corrects a typographical error.
19. 40 CFR 257.100(a)(1); this amendment clarifies that inactive CCR surface impoundments, regardless of how the facility produces electricity through non-fuels, are subject to the same compliance deadlines applicable to existing CCR surface impoundments, subject to certain requirements.
20. 40 CFR 257.104(a); this amendment clarifies that all owners or operators of CCR units that are subject to 40 CFR 257.102 are subject to the post-closure care requirements, except for those owners and operators of a CCR unit that elect to close the CCR unit by removing CCR.

Full answer
-----------

Answer: 0

Reasoning

The five proposed approvals (Wyoming, Virginia, Louisiana, Alabama, and Wisconsin) each discuss the 2024 Legacy Rule amendments that apply to all CCR units. In every document, EPA divides these all‑unit amendments into:

- Those the State has adopted/are being approved – roughly 20 provisions (e.g., the “website” wording update, 40 CFR 257.50(c), 257.52, the updated definitions in 257.53, 257.61(a), 257.80(a), 257.90(a), 257.100(a)(1), 257.104(a), etc.).
- Those that remain federally enforceable because the State did not adopt them – consistently the same 12 “more prescriptive” provisions (the revised “Operator” and “Owner” definitions; 257.80(b)(6); 257.102(c)(2) and (d)(2); 257.102(f)(2)(ii)(C) and (D); 257.104(a)(2), (c)(1) and (3); 257.104(g); 257.105(a) and the record‑retention provisions in 257.105(e), (f), (g), (h) and (i); and 257.107(b) and the website‑retention provisions in 257.107(e), (f), (g), (h) and (i)), plus 257.73/257.74 (vegetative cover), 257.90(g) (groundwater monitoring suspension), 257.95(h)(2) (groundwater standards for appendix‑IV constituents without MCLs), and 257.50(e) (legacy surface impoundments).

Checking each State:

- Wyoming, Virginia, Louisiana, and Alabama all explicitly list those 12 provisions (plus 257.90(g), 257.95(h)(2), 257.73/74 and 257.50(e)) as not being part of their approved programs.
- Wisconsin explicitly lists only 257.73/74, 257.90(g), 257.95(h)(2) and the legacy/CCRMU provisions as remaining federal; Wisconsin’s application does include the other 2024 all‑unit updates (the ~20 provisions listed for approval, including the recordkeeping/notification updates).

So every 2024 all‑unit amendment falls into one of these patterns:

- Missing in 4 States (the 12 “more prescriptive” amendments),
- Missing in all 5 States (257.73/74, 257.90(g), 257.95(h)(2), 257.50(e)), or
- Present in all 5 (the ~20 clarifying/updated provisions that Wisconsin and Wyoming explicitly approve, and that Virginia, Louisiana and Alabama do not list as excluded).

There is no 2024 Legacy Rule all‑unit amendment that is absent from exactly two of the five proposed State programs. Therefore, the count is zero.

Reasoning

The 2024 Legacy Rule made amendments that apply to all CCR units. In the five proposed approvals (Wyoming, Virginia, Louisiana, Alabama, and Wisconsin), each State lists which of those all‑unit provisions it has adopted/interprets and which remain federally enforceable because the State did not adopt them.

- Wyoming lacks only: the legacy‑unit provision (40 CFR 257.50(e)), the vegetative‑cover provisions (40 CFR 257.73/257.74), the groundwater‑monitoring suspension provision (40 CFR 257.90(g)), and the appendix‑IV groundwater standards (40 CFR 257.95(h)(2)).
- Virginia lacks: the same vegetative‑cover, 257.90(g), and 257.95(h)(2) provisions, plus all of the “more prescriptive” 2024 amendments (e.g., the revised “Operator”/“Owner” definitions, 40 CFR 257.80(b)(6), 257.102(c)(2) and (d)(2), 257.104 updates, and the recordkeeping/website retention updates in 257.105 and 257.107).
- Louisiana lacks: the vegetative‑cover provisions, 257.95(h)(2), the variance/exemption provisions, and the same full set of “more prescriptive” 2024 amendments.
- Alabama lacks: the three new definitions (“Contains both CCR and liquids,” “Infiltration,” “Liquids”), 257.71(d), 257.90(g), 257.95(h)(2), and the full set of “more prescriptive” 2024 amendments, but it does include the vegetative‑cover provisions.
- Wisconsin lacks: the vegetative‑cover provisions, 257.90(g), 257.95(h)(2), and the full set of “more prescriptive” 2024 amendments, plus a State‑specific CCR‑pile definition issue.

Counting across all five States for every all‑unit amendment:

- The three new definitions are missing in only Alabama (1 State each).
- 40 CFR 257.71(d) is missing in only Alabama.
- The vegetative‑cover provisions (257.73/257.74) are missing in Wyoming, Virginia, Louisiana, and Wisconsin (4 States).
- 40 CFR 257.90(g) is missing in Wyoming, Virginia, Alabama, and Wisconsin (4 States).
- 40 CFR 257.95(h)(2) is missing in all five States.
- Every “more prescriptive” 2024 amendment (Operator/Owner, 257.80(b)(6), 257.102 updates, 257.104 updates, and the 257.105/257.107 retention/website updates) is missing in Virginia, Louisiana, Alabama, and Wisconsin (4 States each; Wyoming’s program addresses them).

No all‑unit amendment is absent in exactly two States—each is either present everywhere except one State, missing in four States, or missing in all five.

Therefore, the number of 2024 Legacy Rule all‑unit amendments that lack an approved State version in exactly two States is 0.

Let's reproduce that list precisely from Document 3 (around page 23931-23932):

1. Throughout 40 CFR part 257, subpart D, the regulations were amended by removing the phrase "website" and adding in its place the word "website" wherever it appears.
Wait that's typo? Actually "Web site" replaced with "website". Yes.
2. 40 CFR 257.50(c); this amendment revises the scope of applicability to specify that it includes inactive CCR surface impoundments at utilities or power producers regardless of how electricity is currently being produced at the facility.
3. 40 CFR 257.51; this section was reserved, as the effective date of 40 CFR part 257, subpart D, October 19, 2015, has passed.
4. 40 CFR 257.52; this amendment clarifies that all CCR units are subject to the requirement to comply with all other Federal, State, Tribal, or local laws or other requirements. In addition, all CCR units continue to be subject to 40 CFR 257.3-1, 257.3-2, and 257.3-3.
5. "Active facility or active electric utilities or independent power producers"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that the relevant operational date for any active facility or active electric utilities or independent power producers is on or after October 19, 2015.
6. "CCR landfill or landfill"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that a CCR landfill means an area of land or an excavation that "contains", rather than "receives", CCR, and meets the other criteria of the definition.
7. "CCR surface impoundment or impoundment"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 deleted the words "which is".
8. "CCR unit"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that this term includes legacy CCR surface impoundments and CCRMU.
9. "Contains both CCR and liquids"; this additional definition in 40 CFR 257.53 is consistent with the term's plain meaning and dictionary definitions as this term used in the closure performance standard in 40 CFR 257.102(d)(2)(i) for CCR surface impoundments.
10. "Inactive CCR surface impoundment", this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that this term is applicable to such CCR surface impoundments "located at an active facility."
11. "Infiltration"; this additional definition in 40 CFR 257.53 is consistent with the term's plain meaning and dictionary definitions to assist in the application of closure performance standards for CCR units.
12. "Liquids"; this additional definition in 40 CFR 257.53 is consistent with the term's plain meaning and dictionary definitions to assist in the applicability for CCR surface impoundments and the application of closure performance standards for CCR units.
13. "State director"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that the State director is the chief administrative officer of the lead State agency responsible for implementing the State program regulating disposal in all CCR units.
14. "Technically feasible or feasible"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that certain requirements of 40 CFR part 257, subpart D refer only to feasible rather than technically feasible. The amendment ensures that these terms are interpreted in the same way.
15. "Technically infeasible or infeasible"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that certain requirements of 40 CFR part 257, subpart D refer only to infeasible rather than technically infeasible. The amendment ensures that these terms are interpreted in the same way.
16. 40 CFR 257.61(a); this amendment updates a reference to 40 CFR 230.41(a), as the previously referenced provision has since been amended.
17. 40 CFR 257.80(a); this amendment clarifies that all CCR units are subject to the fugitive dust requirements.
18. 40 CFR 257.90(a); this amendment clarifies that all CCR units are subject to the groundwater monitoring and corrective action requirements. In addition, it corrects a typographical error.
19. 40 CFR 257.100(a)(1); this amendment clarifies that inactive CCR surface impoundments, regardless of how the facility produces electricity through non-fuels, are subject to the same compliance deadlines applicable to existing CCR surface impoundments, subject to certain requirements.
20. 40 CFR 257.104(a); this amendment clarifies that all owners or operators of CCR units that are subject to 40 CFR 257.102 are subject to the post-closure care requirements, except for those owners and operators of a CCR unit that elect to close the CCR unit by removing CCR.

Reasoning

The five proposed approvals (Wyoming, Virginia, Louisiana, Alabama, and Wisconsin) each list which 2024 Legacy Rule “all‑unit” amendments are included in the State program and which remain federally enforceable.

- Amendments missing in all five States:
- 40 CFR 257.90(g) (groundwater monitoring suspension)
- 40 CFR 257.95(h)(2) (groundwater standards for Appendix IV constituents with no MCL)
- Amendments missing in four States (WY, VA, LA, WI):
- The 40 CFR 257.73/257.74 vegetative‑cover provisions
- Amendments missing in three States (VA, LA, AL):
- All 12 of the 2024 “more prescriptive” all‑unit amendments (e.g., revised “Operator”/“Owner” definitions; 257.80(b)(6); 257.102(c)(2), (d)(2), (f)(2)(ii)(C)‑(D); 257.104(a)(2),(c)(1),(3), (g); 257.105(a) and retention extensions; 257.107(b) and retention extensions)
- Amendments missing in only one State (AL):
- The three new 2024 definitions: “Contains both CCR and liquids,” “Infiltration,” and “Liquids”
- 40 CFR 257.71(d) (alternate liner demonstration)

No 2024 all‑unit amendment is absent in exactly two of the five proposed State programs; the missing provisions are distributed among 1, 3, 4, or 5 States. Therefore, the number is 0.

- 40 CFR 257.95(h)(2) (groundwater standards for constituents without MCLs) are excluded in Wyoming, Virginia, Louisiana, and Alabama → 4 States
- 40 CFR 257.90(g) (suspension of groundwater monitoring) is listed as excluded only in Wyoming and Virginia; Louisiana, Alabama, and Wisconsin include/adopt it → exactly 2 States

The other 2024 all-unit amendments (e.g., the updated definitions, 257.50(c), 257.80(a), 257.100(a)(1), 257.104(a), etc.) are present in the approved programs of at least three of the five States, and Wisconsin’s additional omissions (e.g., 257.80(b)(6), 257.102(c)(2), 257.104(g), etc.) are shared with Alabama, giving them more than two missing approvals.

So, only one of the 2024 Legacy Rule’s all-unit amendments lacks an approved State version in exactly two States: 40 CFR 257.90(g) (the groundwater-monitoring suspension provision), missing only in Wyoming and Virginia.

Answer: One (40 CFR 257.90(g)).

1. Amendments made in the Legacy CCR Surface Impoundments and CCR Management Units Final Rule. This includes 40 CFR 257.50(e), which WDEQ has not yet adopted.

3. 40 CFR 257.90(g) for suspension of groundwater monitoring;

It is EPA's understanding that VADEQ interprets the provisions in 9VAC20-81-800 the same as EPA interprets these in 40 CFR part 257, subpart D. Therefore, EPA is approving the State's version of the following requirements:
1. Throughout 40 CFR part 257, subpart D, the regulations were amended by removing the phrase "website" and adding in its place the word "website" wherever it appears.
2. 40 CFR 257.50(c); this amendment revises the scope of applicability to specify that it includes inactive CCR surface impoundments at utilities or power producers regardless of how electricity is currently being produced at the facility.
3. 40 CFR 257.51; this section was reserved, as the effective date of 40 CFR part 257, subpart D, October 19, 2015, has passed.
4. 40 CFR 257.52; this amendment clarifies that all CCR units are subject to the requirement to comply with all other Federal, State, Tribal, or local laws or other requirements. In addition, all CCR units continue to be subject to 40 CFR 257.3-1, 257.3-2, and 257.3-3.
5. "Active facility or active electric utilities or independent power producers"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that the relevant operational date for any active facility or active electric utilities or independent power producers is on or after October 19, 2015.
6. "CCR landfill or landfill"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that a CCR landfill means an area of land or an excavation that "contains", rather than "receives", CCR, and meets the other criteria of the definition.
7. "CCR surface impoundment or impoundment"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 deleted the words "which is".
8. "CCR unit"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that this term includes legacy CCR surface impoundments and CCRMU.
9. "Contains both CCR and liquids"; this additional definition in 40 CFR 257.53 is consistent with the term's plain meaning and dictionary definitions as this term used in the closure performance standard in 40 CFR 257.102(d)(2)(i) for CCR surface impoundments.
10. "Inactive CCR surface impoundment", this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that this term is applicable to such CCR surface impoundments "located at an active facility."
11. "Infiltration"; this additional definition in 40 CFR 257.53 is consistent with the term's plain meaning and dictionary definitions to assist in the application of closure performance standards for CCR units.
12. "Liquids"; this additional definition in 40 CFR 257.53 is consistent with the term's plain meaning and dictionary definitions to assist in the applicability for CCR surface impoundments and the application of closure performance standards for CCR units.
13. "State director"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that the State director is the chief administrative officer of the lead State agency responsible for implementing the State program regulating disposal in all CCR units.
14. "Technically feasible or feasible"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that certain requirements of 40 CFR part 257, subpart D refer only to feasible rather than technically feasible. The amendment ensures that these terms are interpreted in the same way.
15. "Technically infeasible or infeasible"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that certain requirements of 40 CFR part 257, subpart D refer only to infeasible rather than technically infeasible. The amendment ensures that these terms are interpreted in the same way.
16. 40 CFR 257.61(a); this amendment updates a reference to 40 CFR 230.41(a), as the previously referenced provision has since been amended.
17. 40 CFR 257.80(a); this amendment clarifies that all CCR units are subject to the fugitive dust requirements.
18. 40 CFR 257.90(a); this amendment clarifies that all CCR units are subject to the groundwater monitoring and corrective action requirements. In addition, it corrects a typographical error.
19. 40 CFR 257.100(a)(1); this amendment clarifies that inactive CCR surface impoundments, regardless of how the facility produces electricity through non-fuels, are subject to the same compliance deadlines applicable to existing CCR surface impoundments, subject to certain requirements.
20. 40 CFR 257.104(a); this amendment clarifies that all owners or operators of CCR units that are subject to 40 CFR 257.102 are subject to the post-closure care requirements, except for those owners and operators of a CCR unit that elect to close the CCR unit by removing CCR."

This appears to be the complete list of "all-unit amendments" from the 2024 Legacy Rule - 20 amendments that apply to all CCR units.

Let me compile the list of "all-unit amendments" from the 2024 Legacy Rule based on the states that have adopted some of them.

From Virginia (Document 3), the 20 all-unit amendments are:
1. Throughout 40 CFR part 257, subpart D, the regulations were amended by removing the phrase "website" and adding in its place the word "website" wherever it appears.
2. 40 CFR 257.50(c); this amendment revises the scope of applicability to specify that it includes inactive CCR surface impoundments at utilities or power producers regardless of how electricity is currently being produced at the facility.
3. 40 CFR 257.51; this section was reserved, as the effective date of 40 CFR part 257, subpart D, October 19, 2015, has passed.
4. 40 CFR 257.52; this amendment clarifies that all CCR units are subject to the requirement to comply with all other Federal, State, Tribal, or local laws or other requirements. In addition, all CCR units continue to be subject to 40 CFR 257.3-1, 257.3-2, and 257.3-3.
5. "Active facility or active electric utilities or independent power producers"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that the relevant operational date for any active facility or active electric utilities or independent power producers is on or after October 19, 2015.
6. "CCR landfill or landfill"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that a CCR landfill means an area of land or an excavation that "contains", rather than "receives", CCR, and meets the other criteria of the definition.
7. "CCR surface impoundment or impoundment"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 deleted the words "which is".
8. "CCR unit"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that this term includes legacy CCR surface impoundments and CCRMU.
9. "Contains both CCR and liquids"; this additional definition in 40 CFR 257.53 is consistent with the term's plain meaning and dictionary definitions as this term used in the closure performance standard in 40 CFR 257.102(d)(2)(i) for CCR surface impoundments.
10. "Inactive CCR surface impoundment", this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that this term is applicable to such CCR surface impoundments "located at an active facility."
11. "Infiltration"; this additional definition in 40 CFR 257.53 is consistent with the term's plain meaning and dictionary definitions to assist in the application of closure performance standards for CCR units.
12. "Liquids"; this additional definition in 40 CFR 257.53 is consistent with the term's plain meaning and dictionary definitions to assist in the applicability for CCR surface impoundments and the application of closure performance standards for CCR units.
13. "State director"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that the State director is the chief administrative officer of the lead State agency responsible for implementing the State program regulating disposal in all CCR units.
14. "Technically feasible or feasible"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that certain requirements of 40 CFR part 257, subpart D refer only to feasible rather than technically feasible. The amendment ensures that these terms are interpreted in the same way.
15. "Technically infeasible or infeasible"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that certain requirements of 40 CFR part 257, subpart D refer only to infeasible rather than technically infeasible. The amendment ensures that these terms are interpreted in the same way.
16. 40 CFR 257.61(a); this amendment updates a reference to 40 CFR 230.41(a), as the previously referenced provision has since been amended.
17. 40 CFR 257.80(a); this amendment clarifies that all CCR units are subject to the fugitive dust requirements.
18. 40 CFR 257.90(a); this amendment clarifies that all CCR units are subject to the groundwater monitoring and corrective action requirements. In addition, it corrects a typographical error.
19. 40 CFR 257.100(a)(1); this amendment clarifies that inactive CCR surface impoundments, regardless of how the facility produces electricity through non-fuels, are subject to the same compliance deadlines applicable to existing CCR surface impoundments, subject to certain requirements.
20. 40 CFR 257.104(a); this amendment clarifies that all owners or operators of CCR units that are subject to 40 CFR 257.102 are subject to the post-closure care requirements, except for those owners and operators of a CCR unit that elect to close the CCR unit by removing CCR.

First, let me compile the complete list of "all-unit amendments" from the 2024 Legacy Rule. The most comprehensive list appears to be in Document 3 (Virginia) with 20 items:

1. Throughout 40 CFR part 257, subpart D, the regulations were amended by removing the phrase "website" and adding in its place the word "website" wherever it appears.
2. 40 CFR 257.50(c); this amendment revises the scope of applicability to specify that it includes inactive CCR surface impoundments at utilities or power producers regardless of how electricity is currently being produced at the facility.
3. 40 CFR 257.51; this section was reserved, as the effective date of 40 CFR part 257, subpart D, October 19, 2015, has passed.
4. 40 CFR 257.52; this amendment clarifies that all CCR units are subject to the requirement to comply with all other Federal, State, Tribal, or local laws or other requirements. In addition, all CCR units continue to be subject to 40 CFR 257.3-1, 257.3-2, and 257.3-3.
5. "Active facility or active electric utilities or independent power producers"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that the relevant operational date for any active facility or active electric utilities or independent power producers is on or after October 19, 2015.
6. "CCR landfill or landfill"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that a CCR landfill means an area of land or an excavation that "contains", rather than "receives", CCR, and meets the other criteria of the definition.
7. "CCR surface impoundment or impoundment"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 deleted the words "which is".
8. "CCR unit"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that this term includes legacy CCR surface impoundments and CCRMU.
9. "Contains both CCR and liquids"; this additional definition in 40 CFR 257.53 is consistent with the term's plain meaning and dictionary definitions as this term used in the closure performance standard in 40 CFR 257.102(d)(2)(i) for CCR surface impoundments.
10. "Inactive CCR surface impoundment", this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that this term is applicable to such CCR surface impoundments "located at an active facility."
11. "Infiltration"; this additional definition in 40 CFR 257.53 is consistent with the term's plain meaning and dictionary definitions to assist in the application of closure performance standards for CCR units.
12. "Liquids"; this additional definition in 40 CFR 257.53 is consistent with the term's plain meaning and dictionary definitions to assist in the applicability for CCR surface impoundments and the application of closure performance standards for CCR units.
13. "State director"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that the State director is the chief administrative officer of the lead State agency responsible for implementing the State program regulating disposal in all CCR units.
14. "Technically feasible or feasible"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that certain requirements of 40 CFR part 257, subpart D refer only to feasible rather than technically feasible. The amendment ensures that these terms are interpreted in the same way.
15. "Technically infeasible or infeasible"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that certain requirements of 40 CFR part 257, subpart D refer only to infeasible rather than technically infeasible. The amendment ensures that these terms are interpreted in the same way.
16. 40 CFR 257.61(a); this amendment updates a reference to 40 CFR 230.41(a), as the previously referenced provision has since been amended.
17. 40 CFR 257.80(a); this amendment clarifies that all CCR units are subject to the fugitive dust requirements.
18. 40 CFR 257.90(a); this amendment clarifies that all CCR units are subject to the groundwater monitoring and corrective action requirements. In addition, it corrects a typographical error.
19. 40 CFR 257.100(a)(1); this amendment clarifies that inactive CCR surface impoundments, regardless of how the facility produces electricity through non-fuels, are subject to the same compliance deadlines applicable to existing CCR surface impoundments, subject to certain requirements.
20. 40 CFR 257.104(a); this amendment clarifies that all owners or operators of CCR units that are subject to 40 CFR 257.102 are subject to the post-closure care requirements, except for those owners and operators of a CCR unit that elect to close the CCR unit by removing CCR.

2. 40 CFR 257.50(c); this amendment revises the scope of applicability to specify that it includes inactive CCR surface impoundments at utilities or power producers regardless of how electricity is currently being produced at the facility.

4. 40 CFR 257.52; this amendment clarifies that all CCR units are subject to the requirement to comply with all other Federal, State, Tribal, or local laws or other requirements. In addition, all CCR units continue to be subject to Sec. Sec. 257.3-1, 257.3-2, and 257.3-3.
5. "Active facility or active electric utilities or independent power producers"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that the relevant operational date for any active facility or active electric utilities or independent power producers is on or after October 19, 2015.
6. "CCR landfill or landfill"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that a CCR landfill means an area of land or an excavation that "contains," rather than "receives," CCR, and meets the other criteria of the definition.
7. "CCR surface impoundment or impoundment"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 deleted the words "which is."
8. "CCR unit"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that this term includes legacy CCR surface impoundments and CCRMU.
9. "Inactive CCR surface impoundment", this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that this term is applicable to inactive CCR surface impoundments "located at an active facility."
10. "State director"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that the State director is the chief administrative officer of the lead State agency responsible for implementing the State program regulating disposal in all CCR units.
11. "Technically feasible or feasible"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that certain requirements of 40 CFR part 257, subpart D refer only to feasible rather than technically feasible. The amendment ensures that these terms are interpreted in the same way.
12. "Technically infeasible or infeasible"; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that certain requirements of 40 CFR part 257, subpart D refer only to infeasible rather than technically infeasible. The amendment ensures that these terms are interpreted in the same way.
13. 40 CFR 257.61(a); this amendment updates a reference to 40 CFR 230.41(a), as the previously referenced provision has since been amended.
14. 40 CFR 257.80(a); this amendment clarifies that all CCR units are subject to the fugitive dust requirements.
15. 40 CFR 257.90(a); this amendment clarifies that all CCR units are subject to the groundwater monitoring and corrective action requirements. In addition, it corrects a typographical error.
16. 40 CFR 257.100(a)(1); this amendment clarifies that inactive CCR surface impoundments, regardless of how the facility produces electricity through non-fuels, are subject to the same compliance deadlines applicable to existing CCR surface impoundments, subject to certain requirements.
17. 40 CFR 257.104(a); this amendment clarifies that all owners or operators of CCR units that are subject to Sec. 257.102 are subject to the post-closure care requirements, except for those owners and operators of a CCR unit that elect to close the CCR unit by removing CCR.

Full answer
-----------

Counting 2024 Legacy Rule All-Unit Amendments Lacking Approved State Versions in Exactly Two States

Identifying the All-Unit Amendments

The 2024 Legacy Rule (89 FR 38985, May 8, 2024) contains 32 all-unit amendments — amendments applying to all CCR units (distinct from legacy impoundment-specific or CCRMU-specific provisions). These split into two groups:

- Group A (20 items): Amendments most States adopted (definitions, scope changes, clarifications to 257.50(c), 257.51, 257.52, various 257.53 definitions, 257.61(a), 257.80(a), 257.90(a), 257.100(a)(1), 257.104(a))
- Group B (12 items): More prescriptive amendments no State adopted ("Operator," "Owner," 257.80(b)(6), 257.102(c)(2), 257.102(d)(2), 257.102(f)(2)(ii)(C)&(D), 257.104(a)(2)/(c)(1)/(3), 257.104(g), 257.105(a), 257.105 retention times, 257.107(b), 257.107 retention times)

State-by-State Coverage of Group A

Wyoming lacks all 20 (covered under "Amendments made in the Legacy CCR Surface Impoundments and CCR Management Units Final Rule" — item 1 of four excluded requirements). Alabama lacks 3 (explicitly not included in its application). Wisconsin lacks 9 (program only covers landfills; several items relate to surface impoundments).

Group B: All 12 items lack approved versions in all 5 States → not exactly 2.

Result

The 10 items with exactly two States lacking an approved version are:

1. 257.50(c) 2. 257.51 3. "CCR surface impoundment" 4. "CCR unit" 5. "Inactive CCR surface impoundment" 6. "Infiltration" 7. "Liquids" 8. "Technically feasible" 9. "Technically infeasible" 10. 257.100(a)(1)

Answer: 10

1. Throughout 40 CFR part 257, subpart D, the regulations were amended by removing the phrase ``Website'' and adding in its place the word ``website'' wherever it appears.

3. ``Active facility or active electric utilities or independent power producers''; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that the relevant operational date for any active facility or active electric utilities or independent power producers is on or after October 19, 2015.

5. ``Infiltration''; this additional definition in 40 CFR 257.53 is consistent with the term's plain meaning and dictionary definitions to assist in the application of closure performance standards for CCR units.
6. ``Liquids''; this additional definition in 40 CFR 257.53 is consistent with the term's plain meaning and dictionary definitions to assist in the applicability for CCR surface impoundments and the application of closure performance standards for CCR units.
7. ``State director''; this amendment to 40 CFR 257.53 clarifies that the State director is the chief administrative officer of the lead State agency responsible for implementing the State program regulating disposal in all CCR units.
8. 40 CFR 257.61(a); this amendment updates a reference to 40 CFR 230.41(a), as the previously referenced provision has since been amended.
9. 40 CFR 257.80(a); this amendment clarifies that all CCR units are subject to the fugitive dust requirements.
10. 40 CFR 257.90(a); this amendment clarifies that all CCR units are subject to the groundwater monitoring and corrective action requirements. In addition, it corrects a typographical error.
11. 40 CFR 257.104(a); this amendment clarifies that all owners or operators of CCR units that are subject to § 257.102 are subject to the post-closure care requirements, except for those owners and operators of a CCR unit that elect to close the CCR unit by removing CCR."

Answer: 3

Preliminary note on the premise

Only four of the five input documents involve EPA proposed approvals of State coal‑ash (CCR) permit programs under RCRA § 4005(d) — Wyoming (Doc. 2), Virginia (Doc. 3), Louisiana (Doc. 4), and Alabama (Doc. 5). Document 1 is an OSMRE final rule approving a Pennsylvania surface‑mining (SMCRA) program amendment; it contains no CCR permit‑program provisions and no 2024 Legacy Rule amendments. The tally below therefore runs across the four actual CCR proposals.

The 2024 Legacy Rule's all-unit amendments (32 total)

As broken out in the Virginia, Louisiana, and Alabama notices, the Legacy Rule's amendments applying to all CCR units fall into two groups:

Group A – clarifying/definitional (20 items) (e.g., "website" wording; 257.50(c); 257.51; 257.52; definitions of "active facility," "CCR landfill," "CCR surface impoundment," "CCR unit," "contains both CCR and liquids," "inactive CCR surface impoundment," "infiltration," "liquids," "State director," "technically feasible," "technically infeasible"; 257.61(a); 257.80(a); 257.90(a); 257.100(a)(1); 257.104(a)).

Group B – more prescriptive (12 items) ("operator," "owner," 257.80(b)(6), 257.102(c)(2), 257.102(d)(2), 257.102(f)(2)(ii)(C)&(D), 257.104(a)(2)/(c)(1)&(3), 257.104(g), 257.105(a), 257.105(e)…, 257.107(b), 257.107(e)…).

State-by-state status

| | Group A (20) | Group B (12) | |---|---|---| | Wyoming | None approved — WDEQ "is not seeking approval for … Amendments made in the Legacy CCR Surface Impoundments and CCR Management Units Final Rule" (all changes WDEQ did not adopt) | None approved | | Virginia | All 20 approved ("EPA is approving the State's version of the following requirements") | None approved (VADEQ did not adopt) | | Louisiana | All 20 approved | None approved | | Alabama | 17 approved; three definitions omitted — "contains both CCR and liquids," "infiltration," "liquids" (listed as not included in the Application) | None approved |

Counting

- Group B (12 items): no approved State version in all four States → not "exactly two."
- 17 of the Group A items: no approved State version in Wyoming only → not "exactly two."
- The three definitions — "contains both CCR and liquids" (257.53), "infiltration" (257.53), and "liquids" (257.53): no approved State version in Wyoming and Alabama (Virginia and Louisiana approved their versions) → exactly two States.

Answer: 3 amendments — the added definitions of "contains both CCR and liquids," "infiltration," and "liquids" at 40 CFR 257.53.

Reasoning
---------

Full answer
-----------

Setting up the comparison

Which documents are EPA coal-ash (CCR) program approvals?

Only four of the five documents are EPA coal-combustion-residuals program approvals: Wyoming (Doc. 2), Virginia (Doc. 3), Louisiana (Doc. 4), and Wisconsin (Doc. 5). (Document 1 is an OSMRE/SMCRA surface-mining amendment for Pennsylvania — a final rule under 30 U.S.C. 1201 et seq., not an EPA CCR permit program.)

What are the 2024 Legacy Rule "all-unit" amendments?

Documents 3 and 4 enumerate them in two groups:

Group 1 (20 items — definitional/clarifying amendments "that apply to all CCR units"): website wording change; 257.50(c); 257.51 (reserved); 257.52; "Active facility…"; "CCR landfill or landfill"; "CCR surface impoundment or impoundment"; "CCR unit"; "Contains both CCR and liquids"; "Inactive CCR surface impoundment"; "Infiltration"; "Liquids"; "State director"; "Technically feasible or feasible"; "Technically infeasible or infeasible"; 257.61(a); 257.80(a); 257.90(a); 257.100(a)(1); 257.104(a).

Group 2 (12 items — all-unit amendments "more prescriptive" than the 2015 rule): "Operator"; "Owner"; 257.80(b)(6); 257.102(c)(2); 257.102(d)(2); 257.102(f)(2)(ii)(C)–(D); 257.104(a)(2),(c)(1)&(3); 257.104(g); 257.105(a); 257.105 record-retention times; 257.107(b); 257.107 record-retention times.

Approval status by State:

- Wyoming – not seeking approval of any 2024 Legacy Rule amendment ("Amendments made in the Legacy CCR Surface Impoundments and CCR Management Units Final Rule… WDEQ has not yet adopted") → all 32 unapproved.
- Virginia – approves all 20 Group 1 items; expressly did not adopt the 12 Group 2 items → 12 unapproved.
- Louisiana – same as Virginia (20 approved, 12 unapproved).
- Wisconsin – landfills-only program; approves only 11 of the 20 Group 1 items (website; 257.52; "Active facility"; "CCR landfill"; "Infiltration"; "Liquids"; "State director"; 257.61(a); 257.80(a); 257.90(a); 257.104(a)); none of the 12 Group 2 items → 21 unapproved.

Counting "exactly two States"

- Group 2 (12 items): unapproved in all four States (WY, VA, LA, WI) → not "exactly two."
- Group 1, the 11 items WI also approved: unapproved only in Wyoming (one State) → not "exactly two."
- Group 1, the 9 items WI did not approve: approved in VA and LA, unapproved in Wyoming and Wisconsin → exactly two States ✔

Those nine are:

1. 40 CFR 257.50(c)
2. 40 CFR 257.51 (reserved)
3. "CCR surface impoundment or impoundment"
4. "CCR unit"
5. "Contains both CCR and liquids"
6. "Inactive CCR surface impoundment"
7. "Technically feasible or feasible"
8. "Technically infeasible or infeasible"
9. 40 CFR 257.100(a)(1)

Full answer
-----------

Identifying Legacy Rule All-Unit Amendments Lacking Approval in Exactly Two States

Setting Up the Analysis

The 2024 Legacy Rule's all-unit amendments (those applying to all CCR units, not legacy-specific or CCRMU-specific) fall into two groups across the four EPA CCR proposals (Wyoming, Virginia, Louisiana, Alabama):

Group A (20 interpretation-matching items): EPA approves the state's version because the state interprets the pre-Legacy Rule text the same as EPA interprets the amended text (e.g., "website" terminology, 257.50(c), 257.51, various definition clarifications).

Group B (12 "more prescriptive" items): Genuinely new requirements added by the Legacy Rule that no state adopted (e.g., expanded "Operator"/"Owner" definitions, 257.80(b)(6), 257.102(c)(2), 257.102(d)(2), retention-time extensions, etc.).

Tracking Approval Status by State

| Item | Wyoming | Virginia | Louisiana | Alabama | |---|---|---|---|---| | Group A (20 items) | ❌ All 20 not approved | ✅ All 20 approved | ✅ All 20 approved | ✅ 17 approved | | Group A: "Contains both CCR and liquids" | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ | | Group A: "Infiltration" | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ | | Group A: "Liquids" | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ | | Group B (12 items) | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ |

Wyoming incorporated federal regulations only through December 14, 2020—before the Legacy Rule—so none of the all-unit amendments have an approved State version.

Virginia and Louisiana are approved for all 20 Group A items (EPA determined they interpret the provisions identically).

Alabama is approved for 17 of the 20 Group A items but not for three definitions it never included in its application (explicitly listed in Alabama's non-approved items): "Contains both CCR and liquids," "Infiltration," and "Liquids."

All 12 Group B items lack approved versions in all four states (not exactly two).

Result

Exactly three all-unit Legacy Rule amendments—the definitions of "Contains both CCR and liquids," "Infiltration," and "Liquids"—lack an approved State version in exactly two States (Wyoming and Alabama).

Full answer
-----------

Looking at this question, I need to identify the 2024 Legacy Rule's all-unit amendments (amendments to provisions applying to all CCR units) and check across the four proposed State CCR program approvals (Virginia, Louisiana, Alabama, Wisconsin) how many lack an approved State version in exactly two States.

Identifying the All-Unit Amendments

Tracking Approval Status Across States
